3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 160.18068pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 160.18068pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 237.53824pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 237.53824pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{1}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where the surjectivity of
r_{p^{k}}
was proved by M. Newmann and J. R. Smart in
[
24
, Thm. 1]
.
Write the associated 7-term exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p)^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k});H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p))}

where,

H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)_{1}:=\ker(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 41.39914pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-41.39914pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.34135pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{res}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.78023pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 52.78023pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces).

Now we show that
H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)_{1}
is zero.

By
[
29
, Lemma 3.7]
and
[
29
, Thm. 3.8]
, we know that, for
k\geq 2
and
g\geq 3
, the groups

H_{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k});\mathbb{Z})
and
H_{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k});\mathbb{Z})
are zero.
Then, by the UCT,

\displaystyle H^{2}(Sp(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p)\simeq

\displaystyle Hom(H_{2}(Sp(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k});\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}/p)

\displaystyle\oplus\text{Ext}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}(H_{1}(Sp(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k});\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}/p)=0,

and

\displaystyle H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p)\simeq

\displaystyle Hom(H_{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}/p)

\displaystyle\oplus\text{Ext}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}(H_{0}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}/p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Hom(\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}),\mathbb{Z}/p)\oplus\text{Ext}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z}/p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p))^{*}.

As a consequence,

H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k});H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p))=H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k});(\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p))^{*}),

and by Lemma
2.5
and Step 2 in
[
29
, Section 4.3]
this last group is zero.

Therefore
H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)_{1}=0
and hence by definition of this group, there is a monomorphism

(A.7)

\res:\;H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)\hookrightarrow H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p).

Next, using this injection we deduce the analogous injection in terms of the mapping class group,
\res:H^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1};\mathbb{Z}/p)\hookrightarrow H^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p^{k}];\mathbb{Z}/p).

Consider the commutative diagram with exact rows:

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{g,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p^{k}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{g,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{g,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1.}

Then there is a ladder of 5-term exact sequences,

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\res}
\scriptstyle{\inf}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1};\mathbb{Z}/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\res}
\scriptstyle{\res}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\inf}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p^{k}];\mathbb{Z}/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\res}

\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathcal{T}_{g,1};\mathbb{Z}/p)^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\res}
\scriptstyle{\inf}
\textstyle{H^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1};\mathbb{Z}/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\res}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathcal{T}_{g,1};\mathbb{Z}/p)^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\inf}
\textstyle{H^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p^{k}];\mathbb{Z}/p)}

Next we prove that the map
\inf:H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p)\rightarrow H^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p^{k}];\mathbb{Z}/p)
is injective and the map
\inf:H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)\rightarrow H^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1};\mathbb{Z}/p)
is an isomorphism. Then by the injection (
A.7
) and a trivial diagram chase we will conclude.

To prove that
\inf:H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p)\rightarrow H^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p^{k}];\mathbb{Z}/p)
is injective we show that
\res:H^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p^{k}],\mathbb{Z}/p)\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathcal{T}_{g,1},\mathbb{Z}/p)^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k})}
is surjective and we will conlude by exact ladder above.
Recall that we denote
H=H_{1}(\Sigma_{g,1};\mathbb{Z})
and
H_{d}=H\otimes\mathbb{Z}/d
for any integer
d
.

By the UCT there are isomorphisms:

\displaystyle H^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p^{k}],\mathbb{Z}/p)\simeq Hom(H_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p^{k}];\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}/p),

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} H^{1}(\mathcal{T}_{g,1},\mathbb{Z}/p)^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k})}\simeq\;&Hom(H_{1}(\mathcal{T}_{g,1};\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}/p)^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k})}\\
\simeq\;&Hom(\Lambda^{3}H_{p^{k}},\mathbb{Z}/p),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}/p)\simeq\;&Hom(H_{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k});\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}/p)\\
\simeq\;&Hom(\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}),\mathbb{Z}/p).\end{aligned}

By
[
35
, Thm. 0.4]
we have a split extension of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}
-modules,

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Lambda^{3}H_{p^{k}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p^{k}];\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Applying the functor
Hom(-,\mathbb{Z}/p),
we get an exact sequence,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Hom(\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}),\mathbb{Z}/p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Hom(H_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p^{k}];\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}/p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Hom(\Lambda^{3}H_{p^{k}},\mathbb{Z}/p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

Therefore,
H^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p^{k}],\mathbb{Z}/p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{res}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(\mathcal{T}_{g,1},\mathbb{Z}/p)^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z},p^{k})}
is surjective.

Next we show that the map
H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{inf}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1};\mathbb{Z}/p)
is an isomorphism.
By the UCT, and the Center kills lemma for the last equality,

\displaystyle H^{1}(\mathcal{T}_{g,1},\mathbb{Z}/p)^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z})}

\displaystyle\simeq Hom(H_{1}(\mathcal{T}_{g,1};\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}/p)^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z})}

\displaystyle\simeq Hom(\Lambda^{3}H_{p},\mathbb{Z}/p)^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z})}=0.

As a consequence, from exact ladder above, the map
H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{inf}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1};\mathbb{Z}/p)
is injective.
Moreover,
[
29
, Thm. 5.1]
and
[
7
, Thm. 5.8]
together with the UCT shows that for
g\geq 4
,
H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}/p)\simeq\mathbb{Z}/p
and
H^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1};\mathbb{Z}/p)\simeq\mathbb{Z}/p
.
Hence the map
inf
is an isomorphism.
∎

Proposition A.5
.

Given an integer
g\geq 2
and an odd prime
p
, the restriction map induces isomorphisms

H^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1};\Lambda^{3}H_{p})\simeq H^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p];\Lambda^{3}H_{p})^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)}\simeq H^{1}(\mathcal{T}_{g,1};\Lambda^{3}H_{p})^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z})}.

Proof.

Consider the following commutative diagram with exact rows:

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{g,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{g,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{g,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}

and the
Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)
-module
\Lambda^{3}H_{p}.

Then there is a ladder of 5-term exact sequences,

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p);\Lambda^{3}H_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1};\Lambda^{3}H_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\Lambda^{3}H_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1};\Lambda^{3}H_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p];\Lambda^{3}H_{p})^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p);\Lambda^{3}H_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1};\Lambda^{3}H_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathcal{T}_{g,1};\Lambda^{3}H_{p})^{Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\Lambda^{3}H_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1};\Lambda^{3}H_{p})}

By the Center kills Lemma, since
-Id
acts on
\Lambda^{3}H_{p}
as the multiplication by
-1,
we have that
H^{i}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p);\Lambda^{3}H_{p})=0=H^{i}(Sp_{2g}(\mathbb{Z});\Lambda^{3}H_{p})
for
i=1,2,
and by exactness we get the result.
∎

Analogously, if we take the handlebody subgroups
\mathcal{B}_{g,1},

\mathcal{B}_{g,1}[p],

\mathcal{TB}_{g,1}
instead of
\mathcal{M}_{g,1},

\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p],

\mathcal{T}_{g,1},
proceeding in the same way that in above Proposition
A.5
we get:

Proposition A.6
.

For any odd prime
p
and an integer
g\geq 2
, the restriction maps induce isomorphisms

H^{1}(\mathcal{B}_{g,1};\Lambda^{3}H_{p})\simeq H^{1}(\mathcal{B}_{g,1}[p];\Lambda^{3}H_{p})^{Sp^{B}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)}\simeq H^{1}(\mathcal{TB}_{g,1};\Lambda^{3}H_{p})^{Sp^{B}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z})}.

We now turn to compute the coinvariant quotients of tensor and exterior powers of the two pieces of
H_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g,1}[p];\mathbb{Z})=\Lambda^{3}H_{p}\oplus\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p),
which we use when computing invariants in Section
5.4
.

For the first piece,
\Lambda^{3}H_{p}
, the exterior power of triples of elements from the symplectic basis of
H_{p}
given by
\{a_{i},b_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq g}
form a basis of the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-vector space
\Lambda^{3}H_{p}
, and then the tensor product of pairs of elements from the basis of
\Lambda^{3}H_{p}
produces a basis of the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-vector space
\Lambda^{3}H_{p}\otimes\Lambda^{3}H_{p}
.

Proposition A.7
.

Given an integer
g\geq 4
and an odd prime
p
, the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-vector space
(\Lambda^{3}H_{p}\otimes\Lambda^{3}H_{p})_{GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})}
is generated by the following six elements:

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge a_{3})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge b_{2}\wedge b_{3})

\displaystyle(b_{1}\wedge b_{2}\wedge b_{3})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge a_{3})

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})

\displaystyle(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge a_{3}\wedge b_{3})

\displaystyle(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge a_{3}\wedge b_{3})

Proof.

Let
c,c^{\prime}
stand for
a
or
b,
and
l,m,n
pair-wise distinct indices.
Notice that the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-vector space
\Lambda^{3}H_{p}\otimes\Lambda^{3}H_{p}
is generated by the
\mathfrak{S}_{g}
-orbits of the following elements:

(c_{1}\wedge c_{2}\wedge c_{3})\otimes(c^{\prime}_{l}\wedge c^{\prime}_{m}\wedge c^{\prime}_{n}),\qquad(c_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(c^{\prime}_{l}\wedge c^{\prime}_{m}\wedge c^{\prime}_{n}),

(c_{1}\wedge c_{2}\wedge c_{3})\otimes(c^{\prime}_{l}\wedge a_{m}\wedge b_{m}),\qquad(c_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(c^{\prime}_{l}\wedge a_{m}\wedge b_{m}).

Taking the quotient by the action of
GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
, we now reduce the number of generators in the coinvariants module
\left(\Lambda^{3}H_{p}\otimes\Lambda^{3}H_{p}\right)_{GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})}.

Let
v
be one of above elements, if
v
has an index
k
that appears an odd number of times, the action by
G=Id-2e_{kk}
sends
v
to
-v,
and hence this element is annihilated in the coinvariants module.
Therefore we are left with:

(c_{1}\wedge c_{2}\wedge c_{3})\otimes(c^{\prime}_{1}\wedge c^{\prime}_{2}\wedge c^{\prime}_{3}),\quad(c_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(c^{\prime}_{1}\wedge a_{m}\wedge b_{m}),

and picking one representative in each
\mathfrak{S}_{g}
-orbit we are left with the generating set:

(c_{1}\wedge c_{2}\wedge c_{3})\otimes(c^{\prime}_{1}\wedge c^{\prime}_{2}\wedge c^{\prime}_{3}),\quad(c_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(c^{\prime}_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2}),

(c_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(c^{\prime}_{1}\wedge a_{3}\wedge b_{3}).

We follow to reduce more the number of generators.

•

For the elements
(c_{1}\wedge c_{2}\wedge c_{3})\otimes(c^{\prime}_{1}\wedge c^{\prime}_{2}\wedge c^{\prime}_{3}),
suppose that
c_{i}=c^{\prime}_{i}
for some
i=1,2,3,
without loss of generality we can suppose
c_{1}=c_{1}^{\prime}.

If
c_{1}=c_{1}^{\prime}=a_{1}
, acting by
G=Id+e_{14}
,

\displaystyle G.(a_{1}\wedge c_{2}\wedge c_{3})\otimes G.(a_{4}\wedge c^{\prime}_{2}\wedge c^{\prime}_{3})=

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge c_{2}\wedge c_{3})\otimes((a_{1}+a_{4})\wedge c^{\prime}_{2}\wedge c^{\prime}_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge c_{2}\wedge c_{3})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge c^{\prime}_{2}\wedge c^{\prime}_{3})

\displaystyle+(a_{1}\wedge c_{2}\wedge c_{3})\otimes(a_{4}\wedge c^{\prime}_{2}\wedge c^{\prime}_{3}).

Then, in the coinvariants module,
(a_{1}\wedge c_{2}\wedge c_{3})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge c^{\prime}_{2}\wedge c^{\prime}_{3})=0.
Analogously, for
c_{1}=c_{1}^{\prime}=b_{1}
, acting by
G=Id+e_{41}
one gets that, in the coinvariants module,
(b_{1}\wedge c_{2}\wedge c_{3})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge c^{\prime}_{2}\wedge c^{\prime}_{3})=0.
Therefore we are left with the elements with
c_{i}\neq c^{\prime}_{i}
for
i=1,2,3,
and picking one representative in each
\mathfrak{S}_{g}
-orbit, we are only left with:

(A.8)

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge a_{3})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge b_{2}\wedge b_{3})

\displaystyle(b_{1}\wedge b_{2}\wedge b_{3})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge a_{3})

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{3})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge b_{2}\wedge a_{3})

\displaystyle(b_{1}\wedge b_{2}\wedge a_{3})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{3})

•

For the elements
(c_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(c^{\prime}_{1}\wedge a_{m}\wedge b_{m})
with
m=2,3
, as in the previous case, if
c_{1}=c_{1}^{\prime}=a_{1},
acting by
G=Id+e_{14}
,

\displaystyle G.(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes G.(a_{4}\wedge a_{m}\wedge b_{m})=

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes((a_{1}+a_{4})\wedge a_{m}\wedge b_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge a_{m}\wedge b_{m})

\displaystyle+(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(a_{4}\wedge a_{m}\wedge b_{m}).

Then, in the coinvariants module,
(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge a_{m}\wedge b_{m})=0.
Analogously, if
c_{1}=c_{1}^{\prime}=b_{1}
, acting by
G=Id+e_{41}
one gets that
(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge a_{m}\wedge b_{m})=0
in the coinvariants module. Therefore we are only left with:

(A.9)

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})

\displaystyle(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge a_{3}\wedge b_{3})

\displaystyle(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge a_{3}\wedge b_{3})

Therefore we only have to treat the elements of (
A.8
) and (
A.9
).

•

Acting by
G=Id+e_{32}
, in the coinvariants module,

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{3})=G.(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes G.(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge(a_{2}+a_{3})\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge(a_{2}+a_{3})\wedge(b_{3}-b_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})+(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge a_{3}\wedge b_{3})

\displaystyle+(a_{1}\wedge a_{3}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{3})).

Thus

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{3})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge b_{2}\wedge a_{3})=

\displaystyle(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge a_{3}\wedge b_{3})

\displaystyle-(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2}).

Analogously, taking the same action we get that

\displaystyle(b_{1}\wedge b_{2}\wedge a_{3})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{3})=

\displaystyle(b_{1}\wedge a_{3}\wedge b_{3})\otimes(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})

\displaystyle-(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\otimes(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2}).

Therefore we are only left with the generating set of the statement.
∎

Taking the antisymmetrization of the generators given in Proposition
A.7
we get the following result:

Corollary A.1
.

Given an integer
g\geq 4
and an odd prime
p
, the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-vector space
(\Lambda^{3}H_{p}\wedge\Lambda^{3}H_{p})_{GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})}
is generated by the following three elements:

(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge a_{3})\wedge(b_{1}\wedge b_{2}\wedge b_{3}),\qquad(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\wedge(b_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2}),

(a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\wedge b_{2})\wedge(b_{1}\wedge a_{3}\wedge b_{3}).

For the second piece,
\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)
,
recall that there is a decomposition as
GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
-modules,

\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)=\mathfrak{gl}_{g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)\oplus Sym^{A}_{g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)\oplus Sym^{B}_{g}(\mathbb{Z}/p),

where the action of
GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
on
\mathfrak{gl}_{g}(\mathbb{Z}/d)
is by conjugation, the action on
Sym^{A}_{g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)
is given by
G.\beta=G\beta\;{}^{t}G

and the action on
Sym^{B}_{g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)
by
G.\gamma={}^{t}G^{-1}\gamma\;G^{-1}
.

Recall that
e_{ij}
denotes the elementary matrix with
1
at the place
ij
and zero elsewhere. Let
i\neq j,
define the following matrices in
\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p):

u_{ij}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&e_{ij}+e_{ji}\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\quad u_{ii}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&e_{ii}\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\quad l_{ij}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
e_{ij}+e_{ji}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\quad l_{ii}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
e_{ii}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),

n_{ij}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}e_{ij}&0\\
0&-e_{ji}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\quad n_{ii}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}e_{ii}&0\\
0&-e_{ii}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

The set formed by these matrices with
1\leq i<j\leq g
is a basis of the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-vector space

\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p).
And then the tensor product of pairs of these matrices produces a basis of the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-vector space
\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)\otimes\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)
.

Proposition A.8
.

Given an integer
g\geq 4
and an odd prime
p
, the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-vector space
(\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)\otimes\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p))_{GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})}
is generated by the following four elements:

n_{11}\otimes n_{11},\quad n_{11}\otimes n_{22},\quad u_{11}\otimes l_{11},\quad l_{11}\otimes u_{11}.

Proof.

Denote by
N,

U,

L
the
GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
-submodules of
\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)
formed by the respective sets of matrices
\{n_{ii},n_{ij}\}
,
\{u_{ii},u_{ij}\}
,
\{l_{ii},l_{ij}\}
with
1\leq i<j\leq g
.
Then the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-vector space
\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)\otimes\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)
is generated by the following
GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
-submodules:

N\otimes N,\quad N\otimes U,\quad N\otimes L,\quad U\otimes N,\quad U\otimes U

U\otimes L,\quad L\otimes N,\quad L\otimes U,\quad L\otimes L.

Taking the quotient by the action of
GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
, we now reduce the number of generators in the coinvariant module
(\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)\otimes\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p))_{GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})}.

•

Reduction of generators in
N\otimes N
.
The submodule
N\otimes N
is generated by the
\mathfrak{S}_{g}
-orbits of the following elements:

\displaystyle n_{11}\otimes n_{11},\quad n_{11}\otimes n_{22},\quad n_{11}\otimes n_{12},\quad n_{11}\otimes n_{21},\quad n_{11}\otimes n_{23},

\displaystyle n_{12}\otimes n_{11},\quad n_{12}\otimes n_{22},\quad n_{12}\otimes n_{12},\quad n_{12}\otimes n_{21},\quad n_{12}\otimes n_{13},

\displaystyle n_{12}\otimes n_{31},\quad n_{12}\otimes n_{23},\quad n_{12}\otimes n_{32},\quad n_{12}\otimes n_{34},\quad n_{12}\otimes n_{33}.

A direct computation shows that the action by
Id-2e_{22}
(resp.
Id-2e_{33}
) sends the generators containing just one element among
\{n_{12},n_{21},n_{23},n_{32}\}
(resp.
\{n_{23},n_{32},n_{34}\}
) to their respective opposite, and hence these elements are annihilated in the coinvariants module. Therefore we are left with:

n_{11}\otimes n_{11},\quad n_{11}\otimes n_{22},\quad n_{12}\otimes n_{12}\quad n_{12}\otimes n_{21}.

We proceed to reduce even more the number of generators.

Using the action by
G_{ij}=Id+e_{ij}
, in the coinvariants module,

(1)

G_{12}.(n_{11}\otimes n_{22})=(n_{11}-n_{12})\otimes(n_{22}+n_{12})=n_{11}\otimes n_{22}-n_{12}\otimes n_{12}.

Then we get that
n_{12}\otimes n_{12}=0.

(2)

\begin{aligned} G_{21}.(n_{11}\otimes n_{12})=&(n_{11}+n_{21})\otimes(-n_{11}+n_{22}+n_{12}-n_{21})=\\[-2.84544pt]
=&-n_{11}\otimes n_{11}+n_{11}\otimes n_{22}+n_{21}\otimes n_{12}.\end{aligned}

Then we get that
n_{21}\otimes n_{12}=n_{11}\otimes n_{11}-n_{11}\otimes n_{22}.

Therefore on
N\otimes N
we are only left with:

n_{11}\otimes n_{11},\quad n_{11}\otimes n_{22}.

•

Reduction of generators in
U\otimes L
and
L\otimes U
.
The group
U\otimes L

is generated by the
\mathfrak{S}_{g}
-orbits of the following elements:

\displaystyle u_{11}\otimes l_{11},\quad u_{11}\otimes l_{22},\quad u_{11}\otimes l_{12},\quad u_{11}\otimes l_{23},\quad u_{12}\otimes l_{11},

\displaystyle u_{12}\otimes l_{33},\quad u_{12}\otimes l_{12},\quad u_{12}\otimes l_{23},\quad u_{12}\otimes l_{34}.

A direct computation shows that the action by
Id-2e_{22}
(resp.
Id-2e_{33}
) sends the generators containing exactly one element among
\{u_{12},u_{23},l_{12},l_{23}\}
(resp.
\{l_{23},l_{34}\}
) to their respective opposite, and hence these elements are annihilated in the coinvariants module. Therefore we are only left with:

u_{11}\otimes l_{11},\quad u_{11}\otimes l_{22},\quad u_{12}\otimes l_{12}.

Using the action by
G_{ij}=Id+e_{ij},
in the coinvariants module,

(1)

G_{12}.(u_{11}\otimes l_{11})=u_{11}\otimes(l_{11}+l_{22}-l_{12})=u_{11}\otimes l_{11}+u_{11}\otimes l_{22}.

Then we get that
u_{11}\otimes l_{22}=0.

(2)

\begin{aligned} G_{21}.(u_{11}\otimes l_{12})&=(u_{11}+u_{22}+u_{12})\otimes(l_{12}-2l_{11})\\
&=u_{12}\otimes l_{12}-2(u_{11}\otimes l_{11})-2(u_{22}\otimes l_{11}).\end{aligned}

Then we get that,

u_{12}\otimes l_{12}=2(u_{11}\otimes l_{11}).

Therefore on
U\otimes L
we are only left with the element
u_{11}\otimes l_{11}.

Analogously on
L\otimes U,
we are only left with the element
l_{11}\otimes u_{11}.

•

Nullity of the generators in
N\otimes U,

N\otimes L,

U\otimes N
and
L\otimes N.
We only prove that the generators in
N\otimes U
vanish in the coinvariants module. The other cases are similar.

The submodule
N\otimes U

is generated by the
\mathfrak{S}_{g}
-orbits of the following elements:

\displaystyle n_{11}\otimes u_{11},\quad n_{11}\otimes u_{22},\quad n_{11}\otimes u_{12}\quad n_{11}\otimes u_{23},

\displaystyle n_{12}\otimes u_{11},\quad n_{12}\otimes u_{22},\quad n_{12}\otimes u_{33},\quad n_{12}\otimes u_{12},

\displaystyle n_{12}\otimes u_{13},\quad n_{12}\otimes u_{23},\quad n_{12}\otimes u_{34}.

A direct computation shows that the action by
Id-2e_{22}
(resp.
Id-2e_{33}
) sends the generators containing just one element among
\{n_{12},u_{12},u_{23}\}
(resp.
\{u_{23},u_{34}\}
) to their respective opposite, and hence these elements are annihilated in the coinvariants module. Therefore we are only left with:

n_{11}\otimes u_{11},\quad n_{11}\otimes u_{22},\quad n_{12}\otimes u_{12}.

We now show that these elements are also annihilated in the coinvariants module.

Using the action by
G_{ij}=Id+e_{ij},
in the coinvariants module,

(1)

\begin{aligned} G_{23}.(n_{11}\otimes u_{33})&=n_{11}\otimes(u_{22}+u_{33}+u_{23})\\
&=n_{11}\otimes u_{22}+n_{11}\otimes u_{33}.\end{aligned}

Then we get that
n_{11}\otimes u_{22}=0.

(2)

\begin{aligned} G_{12}.(n_{11}\otimes u_{22})&=(n_{11}-n_{12})\otimes(u_{11}+u_{22}+u_{12})\\
&=n_{11}\otimes u_{11}-n_{12}\otimes u_{12}.\end{aligned}

Then we get that
n_{11}\otimes u_{11}=n_{12}\otimes u_{12}.

(3)

\begin{aligned} G_{12}.(n_{11}\otimes u_{12})&=(n_{11}-n_{12})\otimes(2u_{11}+u_{12})\\
&=2(n_{11}\otimes u_{11})-n_{12}\otimes u_{12}.\end{aligned}

Then we get that
2(n_{11}\otimes u_{11})=n_{12}\otimes u_{12}
and by the relation obtained in above equation (2) we deduce that
n_{11}\otimes u_{11}=0

and
n_{12}\otimes u_{12}=0
.

•

Nullity of the generators in
U\otimes U
and
L\otimes L.

We prove that the generators in
U\otimes U
vanish in the coinvariants module. The case of
L\otimes L
is analogous.

The submodule
U\otimes U

is generated by the
\mathfrak{S}_{g}
-orbits of the following elements:

\displaystyle u_{11}\otimes u_{11},\quad u_{11}\otimes u_{22},\quad u_{11}\otimes u_{12},\quad u_{11}\otimes u_{23},

\displaystyle u_{12}\otimes u_{11},\quad u_{12}\otimes u_{33},\quad u_{12}\otimes u_{12},\quad u_{12}\otimes u_{13},\quad u_{12}\otimes u_{34}.

A direct computation shows that the action by
Id-2e_{22}
(resp.
Id-2e_{33}
) sends the generators containing just one element among
\{u_{12},u_{23}\}
(resp.
\{u_{23},u_{34}\}
) to their respective opposite, and hence these elements are annihilated in the coinvariants module. Therefore we are only left with:

u_{11}\otimes u_{11},\quad u_{11}\otimes u_{22},\quad u_{12}\otimes u_{12}.

We now show that these elements are also annihilated in the coinvariants module.

Using the action by
G_{ij}=Id+e_{ij},
in the coinvariants module,

(1)

G_{12}.(u_{11}\otimes u_{22})=u_{11}\otimes(u_{11}+u_{22}+u_{12})=u_{11}\otimes u_{22}+u_{11}\otimes u_{11}.

Then we get that
u_{11}\otimes u_{11}=0.

(2)

G_{23}.(u_{11}\otimes u_{33})=u_{11}\otimes(u_{22}+u_{33}+u_{23})=u_{11}\otimes u_{22}+u_{11}\otimes u_{33}.

Then we get that
u_{11}\otimes u_{22}=0.

(3)

G_{21}.(u_{11}\otimes u_{11})=(u_{11}+u_{22}+u_{12})\otimes(u_{11}+u_{22}+u_{12})=u_{12}\otimes u_{12}.

Then we get that
u_{12}\otimes u_{12}=0.

∎

Taking the antisymmetrization of the generators given in Proposition
A.8
we get the following result:

Corollary A.2
.

Given an integer
g\geq 4
and an odd prime
p
, the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-vector space
(\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p)\wedge\mathfrak{sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z}/p))_{GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})}
is generated by the element
u_{11}\wedge l_{11}.

We now compute the
GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
-coinvariants quotient of the algebra
\mathcal{A}_{2}(H_{p})
of uni-trivalent trees with just two trivalent vertices and labels in
H_{p}
, introduced after Proposition
5.9
.

.

Given an integer
g\geq 3
and
p
an odd prime, the group
(\mathcal{A}_{2}(H_{p}))_{GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})}
is generated by the following two elements:

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.68294pt}{-15.59901pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Proof.

To make the notation lighter let us set

H(a,b,c,d):=\hbox to46.06pt{\vbox to37.86pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 23.04778pt\lower-18.93202pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
\par{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -23.62 0 L 23.62 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}

\par
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}(H_{p}).

The module
\mathcal{A}_{2}(H_{p})
is generated by elements of the form

H(c_{i},c^{\prime}_{j},d_{k},d^{\prime}_{l})\qquad\text{with}\qquad\begin{array}[]{l}c,c^{\prime},d,d^{\prime}=a\text{ or }b,\\
i,j,k,l\in\{1,\ldots,g\}\end{array}.

Taking the quotient by the action of
GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
, we now reduce the number of generators in the coinvariant module
(\mathcal{A}_{2}(H_{p}))_{GL_{g}(\mathbb{Z})}
.

Notice that the elements with
i\neq j,k,l
are annihilated by the action of
Id-2e_{i,i}
. This argument also holds for the other coefficients. Therefore picking one element in each
\mathfrak{S}_{g}
-orbit we are left with

H(c_{1},c^{\prime}_{2},d_{2},d^{\prime}_{1}),\quad H(a_{1},b_{1},b_{2},a_{2}),\quad H(a_{1},b_{1},b_{1},a_{1}).

Using the action by
G_{ij}=Id+e_{ij},
we compute:

(1)

G_{13}\cdot H(a_{3},c_{2}^{\prime},d_{2},a_{1})=H(a_{1},c_{2}^{\prime},d_{2},a_{1})+H(a_{3},c_{2}^{\prime},d_{2},a_{1}).

Then we get that

H(a_{1},c_{2}^{\prime},d_{2},a_{1})=0.

(2)

G_{32}\cdot H(c_{1},b_{3},b_{2},d_{1}^{\prime})=H(c_{1},b_{3},b_{2},d_{1}^{\prime})-H(c_{1},b_{2},b_{2},d_{1}^{\prime}).

Then we get that
H(c_{1},b_{2},b_{2},d_{1}^{\prime})=0.

Therefore the set of generators of the form
H(c_{1},c^{\prime}_{2},d_{2},d^{\prime}_{1})
is reduced to the following two elements:

H(b_{1},b_{2},a_{2},a_{1}),\quad H(b_{1},a_{2},b_{2},a_{1}).

(3)

Using the above relations and AS relation we compute:

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle H(a_{1},b_{2},b_{2},a_{1})=G_{21}\cdot H(a_{1},b_{2},b_{2},a_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H(a_{1},b_{1},b_{1},a_{1})-H(a_{1},b_{1},b_{2},a_{2})-H(a_{1},b_{2},b_{1},a_{2})

\displaystyle-H(a_{2},b_{1},b_{2},a_{1})-H(a_{2},b_{2},b_{1},a_{1})+H(a_{2},b_{2},b_{2},a_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2H(a_{1},b_{1},b_{1},a_{1})-2H(a_{1},b_{1},b_{2},a_{2})-2H(a_{1},b_{2},b_{1},a_{2}).

Then dividing by
2
we get that,

\displaystyle H(a_{1},b_{1},b_{1},a_{1})=

\displaystyle H(a_{1},b_{1},b_{2},a_{2})+H(a_{1},b_{2},b_{1},a_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H(a_{1},b_{1},b_{2},a_{2})-H(b_{1},a_{2},b_{2},a_{1}).

(4)

Using the IHX, AS relations we get the following identity:

\displaystyle H(b_{1},a_{2},b_{2},a_{1})=

\displaystyle H(b_{1},b_{2},a_{2},a_{1})+H(a_{2},b_{2},a_{1},b_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H(b_{1},b_{2},a_{2},a_{1})-H(a_{1},b_{1},b_{2},a_{2}).

∎
